    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. The exec-only reward creates a pure reward hacking incentive: the model discovers that contradictions + trivially true conclusions always satisfy the prover.\\\\n\\\\n## Implication for paper:\\\\nExtended training is a clean ablation showing exec-only reward's failure mode worsens over time. _source line 58 · 2026-05-15T19:03:21.002Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have a clear picture. The worker "worker_analysis_h7-convergence-timing" was analyzing H7 convergence timing patterns. Looking at the decisions D087-D094, the key evolution is:

1. D087: Initially H7 was thought to be "GRPO universal convergence endpoint" (Random at 1000 steps got 100% H7)
2. D088: Corrected - exec-only 1000 steps is 100% H1, not H7. H7 is additive reward specific
3. D089: Further correction - exec-only is seed-dependent (s42=H1, s43=H7, s44=H1+H8)


4. D090-D091: Confirmed Goodhart collapse across seeds, with s44-1000 dropping from 3% to 0%
5. D092-D093: Random s43-r3 showed H7 at 38.3%, contradicting the earlier "Random H7=0%" assumption
6. D094: Final resolution - H7a is GRPO universal but seed-dependent, while H7b is ISR-specific

The analysis ultimately pinpointed how H7 convergence varies by seed and training method, with the worker's findings supporting the distinction between H7a and H7b behaviors.

Now I'm checking the registry metrics against the documented decisions to verify consistency, particularly the H7 convergence timing across Random and ISR conditions at different step counts, and whether the cross-seed patterns hold up in the actual data.
```

2. `plan001-exec-only-s43-r4`: This is the 500步 exec-only s43, but its metrics are eval_sampling/greedy results (PR=0.14), not the H7 taxonomy data I was looking for

Now let me check the GPU processes and verify the RUNNING experiment.

Let me now check key questions:


1. **D094 claim that H7a is GRPO universal seed-dependent**: Looking at the results across different seeds and step counts, I'm seeing a clear pattern where H7 performance varies dramatically by seed — exec-only s42 shows H7=0 while s43 shows H7=100%, and the random variants show H7 ranging from 0 to 100% depending on the seed and step count, confirming the seed-dependent behavior.

2. **H7 convergence timing**: The ISR results show H7 reaching 97-100% at 1000 steps across seeds 42-44, with particularly fast convergence at 500 steps for s43 and s44, whereas random seed variants show more variable convergence patterns — s42 jumps from 0% to 100% between 500 and 1000 steps while s43 sits at 38.3% at 500 steps. To properly test whether ISR accelerates H7 convergence, I'd need the 500-step H7 rate for ISR s42 to compare against the random variant's trajectory.

Looking at the available data, ISR s43 and s44 both hit 100% H7 by 500 steps while random s42 and s43 are at 0% and 38.3% respectively at that checkpoint, which does suggest ISR amplifies H7 emergence. I'm trying to locate the missing ISR s42 500-step data point to complete this comparison.

Now I'm noticing something odd in the GPU allocation — GPU 0 shows completely idle (0%, 0 MiB) while s44-r2 is supposedly running, but GPU 1 is at 87%, so the job might actually be executing on GPU 1 despite what the roadmap indicates about GPU 0 assignment.

There's a discrepancy though — the ROADMAP indicates plan018-s44-r2 should trigger on GPU 0 once it's freed, but the actual submission routed it to GPU 1 instead.

Now let me look at the convergence patterns across the H7 results. The ISR additive experiments show dramatic improvement with more steps — s42 jumps from 16% to nearly 97% when doubling from 500 to 1000 steps, then s43 and s44 both hit 100% at 500 steps. The random additive variant tells a different story though, starting at 0% for s42.

500 but then jumping to 100% at s42.1000, while s43 shows only partial convergence at 38%. The exec-only condition is more fragmented — s42 never produces H7 solutions even with 1000 steps, s43 suddenly achieves 100% H7 at 1000 steps, and s44 shows a split between H1 and H8 solutions rather than H7. I'm starting to see that convergence timing varies significantly depending on the strategy and seed combination. now clear**: ISR achieves high H7 faster (500步), Random takes longer (visible at 500步 for s43 but not for s42), and exec-only is seed-dependent at 1000步.

2. **D094 conclusion validity**: "H7a is GRPO universal seed-dependent, H7b is ISR-specific" — supported by:
   - Exec-only s43-1000: 100% H7a (no ISR bonus)
   - Random s43: 38.3% H7a (no ISR bonus)
   - Only ISR s44 has H7b
   ✓ This conclusion appears well-supported.

3. **"ISR amplifies H7"** claim validity:
   - ISR s43 500步: 100% H7 vs Random s43 500步: 38.3% H7 → ISR amplification ✓
   - ISR s44 500步: 100% H7 vs Random s44: TBD (RUNNING)
   - ISR s42 500步: 16% H7 vs Random s42 500步: 0% H7 → ISR amplification ✓
   - At 1000步 both approaches converge to ~100% H7 for some seeds, so the amplification claim really hinges on ISR's speed advantage in reaching saturation earlier.

4. **Potential concerns**: The s42 seed shows unusually low H7 performance at 500步 (16%) compared to s43 and s44 (both 100%), which suggests significant inter-seed variance that the analysis should account for.

5. **Genuine rate inconsistencies across checkpoints**: s42 shows conflicting genuine classifications—ISR at 500步 had 14 genuine samples (17.3%), but dropped to 2 (2.2%) at 1000步, while s43 and s44 were already at 0% genuine by 500步. There's also a discrepancy with exec-only s42 where a 500步 genuine count of 7 was later flagged as incorrect in the 1000步 reclassification, raising questions about whether the 500步 samples were genuinely misclassified or if the later reclassification was the error.

6. **Cross-seed variance is substantial**: Random s42 at 500步 shows 0% H7 while Random s43 hits 38.3% H7—this extreme variance confirms that single-seed conclusions are unreliable, which the analysis already acknowledges.

7. **Critical gaps**: Random s44 at 500步 is still running and would complete the picture, and there's no 1000步 data for ISR or Random at s43/s44.

8. **Convergence timing conclusions**: The worker's analysis suggests genuine collapse is seed-invariant across all conditions at sufficient steps, hacking mode is seed-dependent, and ISR accelerates H7 relative to Random.

The analysis actually traces a thoughtful evolution—starting from "H7 is universal GRPO endpoint" through "exec-only is H1" to "actually seed-dependent" to finally "H7a is universal seed-dependent, H7b is ISR-specific." Looking at the convergence data: ISR at 500步 shows 2 out of 3 seeds already saturated at high H7 levels, while Random at 500步 is much lower and more variable, suggesting ISR genuinely accelerates H7 emergence compared to the baseline.

But examining the individual seed trajectories reveals something interesting—ISR's s42 sits at only 16% H7 at 500步, which is surprisingly low compared to s43 and s44 that are already near saturation. By 1000步, s42 jumps to 96.7%, indicating the convergence window for this seed is actually between 500-1000步, whereas s43 and s44 converged before 500步. So ISR shows variable convergence timing across seeds rather than uniform acceleration.

For Random, s42 goes from 0% at 500步 to 100% at 1000步, and s43 is stuck at 38.3% with no later data point. This makes the "ISR accelerates" claim more nuanced—ISR does have an edge at 500步 for s42 (16% vs 0%), but both methods eventually reach near-complete convergence, just on different timelines depending on the seed.

I'm wondering what the worker_analysis_h7-convergence-timing actually produced as output, since the name suggests it was specifically analyzing this convergence pattern. I should look for any analysis artifacts or check what got captured in the decision records around that time.

Training ISR trajectories show monotonic acceleration toward saturation (ISR→1.0) under ISR conditions, a pattern absent in Random (where H7 receives no differential reward) and inconsistent in Exec-only (where H7 emerges in only 1/3 seeds by 1000 steps).\\\"\\n\\n**What NOT to claim without more data**: Specific step numbers for \\\"H7 first appearance\\\" (we have ISR proxy, not taxonomy at each step). Also avoid implying ISR *causes* H7 — it amplifies and accelerates an already-discoverable pattern.\\n\\n## 7. This would give direct H7 count timelines instead of ISR proxy.\\n\\n2. **Random s44 completion**: Currently running on cuda:1. Wait for completion and taxonomy eval to complete the Random 3-seed picture.\\n\\n3. **Random s42 1000-step clean run**: The existing 1000-step log is contaminated by resume.
